# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 2–34

## Page Front Pastedown — IIIF Canvas #2

**Folio / Side:** verso (inside front board) · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0002.JP2` (5508 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0002.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0002.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Inside front cover pastedown containing modern pencil cataloguing inscription (identifying MS. 990 as an Austro-Bavarian Franciscan medical and surgical compendium, c. 1675) and the pasted bookplate of the Wellcome Historical Medical Library (Accession No. 41913).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Wellcome Historical Medical Library Bookplate (Acc. 41913, MS.990)** (`#xywh=620,2880,2300,3470`): Printed red-and-black heraldic ex-libris of the Wellcome Historical Medical Library with manuscript blue-ink accession number '41913' and shelfmark 'MS.990'.

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 767 = MS 990 | 767 = MS 990 *— Note: Former shelfmark 767 equated to Wellcome MS.990.* |
| 2 | [ME] ARZNEIBUCH | [ME] PHARMACOPOEIA / MEDICAL COMPENDIUM [Arzneibuch] *— Note: Faint erased 'ME' before capital pencil title 'ARZNEIBUCH'.* |
| 3 | Compendium of medicine and surgery. | Compendium of medicine and surgery. *— Note: 20th-century English cataloguer's hand (pencil).* |
| 4 | Compiled for the use of a Community | Compiled for the use of a Community |
| 5 | of the Franciscan Order probably in | of the Franciscan Order probably in |
| 6 | Austria or South Germany | Austria or South Germany |
| 7 | [c. 1675] | [c. 1675] *— Note: Estimated date of compilation.* |

## Page [29] — IIIF Canvas #7

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0007.JP2` (5012 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0007.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0007.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

First extant text page of the Arzneibuch ([p. 29], recto preceding p. 30; pages 1–28 wanting). Written in double column within red rules with red rubrics. Contains iatrochemical and folk-medical recipes using eggshells (for urinary calculus, hemorrhage, proud flesh), chimney soot (for cancer and plague anthrax), and sourdough bread (pharmacological properties, maturing poultice, febrile headache, inflamed eyes, post-febrile halitosis, and sustaining life by the aroma of bread).

### Column A (Left Column, within red rules X=240..2656, Y=314..6464)

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | **[Rubric] Für Sandt vnd stain.** | **[Rubric] For sand and stone [urinary gravel and calculus].** *— Note: Red rubric; upper right margin of col. A slightly abraded.* |
| 2 | ℞ die aierschallen Von denen Jungen außgebro= | Take [℞] the eggshells from which young chicks have hatched *— Note: ℞ = Recipe.* |
| 3 | chenen hienlen, das weiche weise häutl thüe | out, remove the soft white inner membrane, *— Note: hienlen = Hühnlein (chicks); häutl = Häutlein.* |
| 4 | hin wekh mach die schallen zu ꝑll̵ [Pulver], vnd nimb ℨj | make the shells into powder [ꝑll̵], and take 1 drachm [ℨj] *— Note: ꝑll̵ = Pulver (powder); ℨj = 1 drachm.* |
| 5 | in V̈ [Wein], ist ein khöstliches mitl wider den sandt | in wine [V̈]; it is a precious remedy against sand, *— Note: V̈ = Vinum / Wein.* |
| 6 | stain vnd verstopfften ⊡ [Harn]. | stone, and obstructed urine [⊡]. *— Note: ⊡ = Urina / Harn (strangury / urinary retention).* |
| 7 | andere Nemen ein Jede aierschallen vnd richten sÿe | Others take any eggshell and strip out |
| 8 | das weisse auß. | the white [inner lining] from it. |
| 9 | **[Rubric] Blüett stöllen.** | **[Rubric] To staunch blood [Hemostasis].** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 10 | ℞. waiche aierschallen in ✚ [Essig] biß sÿe waich | Take [℞] and soak eggshells in vinegar [✚] until they become *— Note: ✚ = Acetum / Essig (vinegar).* |
| 11 | werden, hernach drikhnes an der ☉ [Sonne] vnd machs | soft; afterwards dry them in the sun [☉] and make them *— Note: ☉ = Sol / Sonne.* |
| 12 | zu ꝑll̵ [Pulver], vnd sträe es auf ein blüett fluß, es | into powder [ꝑll̵], and strew it upon a hemorrhage, wherever |
| 13 | seÿ wo es will, so stilt es von stund an. | it may be, and it staunches it immediately. *— Note: von stund an = instantaneously.* |
| 14 | die schallen allein zu ✗ [Kalk/Pulver] gemacht, vnd auf die | The shells alone calcined to powder [✗] and strewed upon *— Note: ✗ = calcined powder / lime.* |
| 15 | fliesente gulden ader gesträet so stehts. | bleeding hemorrhoids [fließende goldene Ader] stops the flow. *— Note: gulden ader = vena aurea (hemorrhoidal vein).* |
| 16 | Zu dem erhizten Mänlichen glidt, khoche aier | For the inflamed male member, boil egg- |
| 17 | schallen vnd lehm in guetten weisen V̈ [Wein], vnd bahe | shells and clay in good white wine [V̈], and foment *— Note: bahe = bathe/foment (warm compress).* |
| 18 | dich darmit so wirst wunder erfahren. | yourself therewith, and you will experience wonders. |
| 19 | **[Rubric] Özen.** Präne aierschallen in offen das sÿe weis | **[Rubric] Cauterizing [Escharotic].** Burn eggshells in the furnace until they turn *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Özen' (= Ätzen).* |
| 20 | werden, dises ꝑll̵ [Pulver] dient zum özen das | white; this powder [ꝑll̵] serves for eating away *— Note: Quicklime (CaO) produced by calcining eggshells.* |
| 21 | wilde fleisch. | proud flesh [exuberant granulation tissue]. *— Note: wildes Fleisch = caro luxurians.* |
| 22 | **[Rubric] Für den khrebs —** | **[Rubric] For cancer [ulcerated carcinoma / phagedenic ulcer] —** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 23 | ℞ ruess auß dem rauchfang, wain leger | Take [℞] soot out of the chimney, wine-lees, *— Note: wain leger = Weinleger (wine lees / tartar).* |
| 24 | Eisen rost, gaiss khääß, mach auß allen ein | iron rust, [and] goat's cheese; make out of them all a |
| 25 | Pflaster legs über den khrebs 48 stundt, | plaster, lay it over the cancer for 48 hours, |
| 26 | so stirbt der khrebs, oder wenigist halt | and the cancer dies, or at the very least one |
| 27 | man ihn darmit auf, vnd benimbt ihm | arrests it therewith and deprives it of |
| 28 | sein bosshait. ‹volgentes› | its malignancy. ‹the following› *— Note: 'volgentes' added supralinearly above 'obgemeltes' in l. 29.* |
| 29 | **[Rubric] Pöß.** in der Pöß, ist ~~obgemeltes~~ Pflaster | **[Rubric] Plague.** In the plague, ~~the aforesaid~~ ‹the following› plaster is *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Pöß.' (= Pest); 'obgemeltes' struck through.* |
| 30 | ein hörliches mitl für den antrax, Nimb | a glorious remedy for the carbuncle [anthrax]: Take *— Note: antrax = plague carbuncle / bubo.* |
| 31 | den härten ruess auß den Camin, ℥x. | the hard soot out of the chimney, 10 ounces [℥x], |
| 32 | Saur daig, Therpentin, vnd Putter āā ℥j. | sourdough, turpentine, and butter, of each 1 ounce [āā ℥j], *— Note: āā = ana (of each an equal quantity).* |
| 33 | Venedische saiffen ℥iß., 3 aier dotter. | Venetian soap 1½ ounces [℥iß], 3 egg yolks, |
| 34 | Theriac, Medridat, āā ℨij. stoss in Morser | theriac [and] mithridatium, of each 2 drachms [āā ℨij]; pound in a mortar *— Note: Classic alexipharmic electuaries.* |
| 35 | wider ein ander zu einen Pflaster. | together into a plaster. |
| 36 | **[Rubric] Saur daig khrafft —** | **[Rubric] Virtue of sourdough —** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 37 | der saur daig hat ein vermischte aigenschafft | Sourdough possesses a mixed [galenic] quality |
| 38 | einer Temperierten wärme vnd zu khielen: | of a temperate warmth and of cooling, |
| 39 | also das er nit zu vill khielt vnd nit zu vill | such that it neither cools too much nor heats |
| 40 | wärmet. er resoluieret, zeittiget, auch zu er | too much; it resolves, ripens [matures], and also [serves] to *— Note: Continues across column break to col. B, l. 1 ('waichen'). Quire mark '2.' below.* |

### Column B (Right Column, within red rules X=2656..4777, Y=314..6464)

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | waichen, Saur daig mit geschmalznen | soften [emolliate]. Sourdough mixed with lard/greased *— Note: Completes 'zu er-waichen' from col. A, l. 40.* |
| 2 | öl vermischt, gibt ein guettes maturier | oil yields a good maturating [suppurative] |
| 3 | Sälbel. Saur daig in Prandt wein ver= | ointment. Sourdough mixed in brandy [aqua vitae] *— Note: Prandt wein = Branntwein.* |
| 4 | mischt vnd über geschlagen stillet alle schmer= | and applied as a poultice relieves all pains |
| 5 | zen von khälte her khumen. | that arise from cold. |
| 6 | **[Rubric] Haubtwehe.** in hizigen fiebern vnd andern | **[Rubric] Headache.** In ardent fevers and other *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Haubtwehe.'* |
| 7 | khrankheiten wo grose haubt wehe seindt | illnesses where there are severe headaches, |
| 8 | wie auch in der gelbsucht, nimb saur daig | as also in jaundice, take sourdough, |
| 9 | ℥v, oder 6, stöss khrenns wurzel ꝑll̵ [Pulver] ℨv | 5 or 6 ounces [℥v–vj], pound horseradish root powder [ꝑll̵] 5 drachms [ℨv], *— Note: khrenns wurzel = Krenwurzel (Armoracia rusticana, rubefacient).* |
| 10 | holler Essig, oder andern ✚ [Essig] zu einen | elder-vinegar, or other vinegar [✚], made into a *— Note: holler Essig = Sambucus nigra vinegar.* |
| 11 | muess gemacht, wasche dem khrankhen vor= | poultice-mash; first wash the patient's |
| 12 | hero die füess woll mit warmen V̈ [Wein], oder | feet well with warm wine [V̈], or *— Note: Note direct contrast between V̈ (wine) and 🜔▽ (salt water).* |
| 13 | 🜔▽ [Salzwasser], als dan legs über, vnd so es drukhen | salt water [🜔▽], then apply it [to the soles], and when it dries *— Note: Galenic revulsive cataplasm to draw humors down from the head.* |
| 14 | legs wider umb über. | apply it afresh. |
| 15 | Saur daig mit 🜔 [Salz] vnd öl vermischt, waichet | Sourdough mixed with salt [🜔] and oil softens |
| 16 | die Pestilenzische Blattern, vnd harte | pestilential pustules and hard |
| 17 | Päulen, vnd öffnets baldt. | boils [buboes], and opens them quickly. *— Note: Päulen = Beulen.* |
| 18 | Saur daig mit scharffer laugen vermischt | Sourdough mixed with sharp lye |
| 19 | vnd über faulle löcher gelegt, zicht alle | and laid over putrid ulcers draws out all |
| 20 | vnrainigkhait herauß. | impurity. |
| 21 | **[Rubric] Erhizte augen.** ℞. Prot von rokhen | **[Rubric] Inflamed eyes.** Take [℞] bread of rye *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Erhizte augen.'* |
| 22 | oder waizen, waiche es in rosen ▽ [Wasser], | or wheat, steep it in rose-water [▽] [and] |
| 23 | frische milch, biß woll angesogen, drukh | fresh milk until well soaked, press it |
| 24 | ein wenig auß, vnd bindts über die erhizten | out a little, and bind it over the inflamed |
| 25 | augen. | eyes. |
| 26 | **[Rubric] Stinkhenten Mund nach einer khrankheit** | **[Rubric] Fetid breath [halitosis] after an illness** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 27 | Wan nach einen fieber od[er] andern khrank= | When after a fever or other illness |
| 28 | heit, der mund schmäkht, so Nimb khalte | the mouth smells foul, take cold *— Note: Austro-Bavarian 'schmäkht' = smells/stinks.* |
| 29 | Prun ▽ [Wasser] halbe voll, lege darein rokhen | spring-water [▽] half full, lay rye |
| 30 | Prodt, vnd Esse es darauß, souill er mag | bread therein, and let him eat thereof as much as he can; |
| 31 | thue es 3 tag nach ein ander nüchter, | do this for 3 days in succession fasting, |
| 32 | wird der mund woll riechendt werden | and the mouth will become sweet-smelling. |
| 33 | Welliche khrankhe sehr Verzehrt vnd nit Essen | Those patients who are greatly wasted and cannot |
| 34 | khünen, sollen an frischen Prodt offter mal | eat should frequently smell fresh bread; |
| 35 | riechen, es erhalt den Menschen Natirlicher | it naturally sustains a person |
| 36 | weiss ein Zeit lang beÿ dem leben, wie | alive for a time, just as |
| 37 | dan einer 3 Jahr [Tag] sein leben also erhalten | one man thus preserved his life for 3 years [days]; *— Note: Allusion to Democritus sustaining life for 3 days by the smell of hot bread.* |
| 38 | andere legens in Maluasier geweikht | others lay it steeped in Malmsey wine *— Note: Maluasier = Malvasia wine.* |
| 39 | aufs herz. der stotts gersten Prodt Ißet | upon the heart. Whoever constantly eats barley bread |
| 40 | Soll Von Podagra vnd dergleichen füessen | shall be freed from podagra [gout] and similar |
| 41 | khrankheiten befreiet sein — | diseases of the feet — |

## Page 30 — IIIF Canvas #8

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0008.JP2` (5425 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0008.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0008.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 30 (verso, numbered '30' in upper-left margin; first numbered page cited in Wellcome catalogue 'pages 30-790'). Contains recipes for colic (Grimen), stiffening of the nerves (Nerven Erstarrung), pleurisy (Seiden stechen), plague prophylaxis (Pöß), the folk pediatric 'heart-worm or goat-worm' tested via a live gudgeon fish bound to the navel (Gaiß Würm den khindern), joint-water discharge (Glidt Wasser), consumption (Dörsucht / Dörsichtigen), snail oil for wasted limbs (Schwinden der glider), cough and hemoptysis (Hüesten vnd außwurff), and plague/poisoned wounds.

### Column A (Left Column, within red rules X=575..2776, Y=274..6498)

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | [30 \|] **[Rubric] Grimen.** Nime ein [schniten] | [30 \|] **[Rubric] Colic [Griping of the bowels].** Take a [slice of] *— Note: Page number '30' in upper-left corner box; inline red rubric 'Grimen.'* |
| 2 | haissen Waizenbrodt, vnd über den Nabel des | hot wheat-bread, and bound over the navel of the |
| 3 | khrankhen gebunden, benimbt also baldt das | patient, it immediately takes away the |
| 4 | grimen. **[Rubric] Nerven Erstarrung —** | colic. **[Rubric] Stiffening of the nerves [sinews] —** *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Nerven Erstarrung —'.* |
| 5 | Nüß öll mit holler blüe infundiert, ist | Walnut oil infused with elder-blossoms is |
| 6 | khöstlich zu den ein geschrumpfften Nerven. | precious for shrunken [contracted] nerves/sinews; |
| 7 | es Zertheilt die harte geschwulst, heilt alle | it disperses hard swelling [and] heals all |
| 8 | laidt gifftige wunden. | kinds of venomous wounds. *— Note: laidt / allerhandt gifftige wunden.* |
| 9 | **[Rubric] Seiden stechen.** das kreüz in der | **[Rubric] Pleurisy [Stitch in the side].** The woody cross [septum] inside the *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Seiden stechen'; woodyJuglans regia septum (signatura rerum).* |
| 10 | Nüß zu ꝑll̵ [Pulver] gemacht. ℨj ein geben hilfft | walnut made into powder [ꝑll̵], 1 drachm [ℨj] administered, helps |
| 11 | für seiden stechen. **[Rubric] Pöß.** Nimb 2 gedörte | for pleurisy. **[Rubric] Plague.** Take 2 dried *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Pöß.' (= Pest).* |
| 12 | nüß mit 2 feigen, vnd 20 rauten Plätlein | walnuts with 2 figs, and 20 rue leaves [Ruta graveolens], *— Note: Famous Mithridatic plague prophylactic.* |
| 13 | vnd ein wenig 🜔 [Salz], vnder ein ander gestosen, | and a little salt [🜔], pounded together, |
| 14 | vnd nüchter ein genomen, preseruiert vor | and taken fasting, it preserves against |
| 15 | der Pöst. — | the plague. — |
| 16 | **[Rubric] Gaiß Würm den khindern —** | **[Rubric] Goat-worms [Heart-worms] in children —** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 17 | Die khinder, so den herz oder gaiß wurm | Children who have the heart-worm or goat-worm, *— Note: Early Modern folk pediatric diagnosis for marasmus/infantile wasting.* |
| 18 | haben, stättigs schreien, vnd am leib abnemen | who cry continually and waste away in body, |
| 19 | wird geholffen, wan man Venedisches glas | are helped when one takes Venetian glass |
| 20 | mit wenig habern, zu ✗ [Pulver] macht, hernach | with a little oats, makes it into fine powder [✗], afterwards |
| 21 | mit hönig Vermischt, vnd den khind in einer | mixes it with honey, and binds it upon the child in a |
| 22 | Nußschallen über den Nabel bindt, so khumbt | walnut-shell over the navel: then comes |
| 23 | als der wurm auß begierde zum hönig | the worm out of craving for the honey |
| 24 | mit dem khopf auß den Nabel her für vnd | forth with its head out of the navel and |
| 25 | frißt darvon, vnd wirdt also durch das glas | eats thereof, and is thus killed by the glass. |
| 26 | erdött. das aber das khind den wurm | That the child truly has the worm, |
| 27 | habe ist nit gnueg das es stätts schreÿe, vnd | however, it is not enough that it continually cries and |
| 28 | abnembe, sondern es wirdt also probieret | wastes away, but it is tested in this manner: |
| 29 | man nimbt ein lebendige grundl, vnd | one takes a live gudgeon [Gobio gobio / loach], and *— Note: grundl = Grundel / Bartgrundel (small freshwater fish).* |
| 30 | bind es dem khind über das Näbl, laß es | binds it upon the child over the navel; leave it |
| 31 | 3, 4 oder 8 stund darauff, wans ein | for 3, 4, or 8 hours thereon; if it has a |
| 32 | wurm hat, so sicht mans wan mans | worm, one sees when one unbinds |
| 33 | auf bind, das die grundl angnagdt, oder | it that the gudgeon has been gnawed at, or |
| 34 | schier halb Verzehrt, als dan legt man | almost half consumed; only then does one apply |
| 35 | erst das glas vnd hönig auf. vnd so man | the glass and honey. And when one |
| 36 | sicht, das der wurm auch darvon gef= | sees that the worm has also eaten thereof, |
| 37 | ressen, gibt man dem khind gequöksilber= | one gives the child mercury-infused *— Note: gequöksilbertes wasser = aqua mercurialis (water boiled over metallic mercury).* |
| 38 | tes wasser 2 oder 3 tag in khöchel, das | water for 2 or 3 days in its pap [porridge], which *— Note: khöchel = Köchel (infant pap/gruel).* |
| 39 | treibt den todten stükh weiss auß. | expels the dead [worm] piecemeal. |
| 40 | **[Rubric] Glidt Wasser —** schnekhen zu aschen | **[Rubric] Joint-water [Synovial discharge] —** Snails burned *— Note: Inline red rubric 'Glidt Wasser —' (synovia escaping from deep joint wounds).* |
| 41 | gebrent, vnd in die wunden gesträet, stölt | to ash and strewed into the wounds staunches |
| 42 | das glidt wasser, khräfftigkhlich. | the joint-water powerfully. |
| 43 | **[Rubric] Dörsucht.** Wasser schnekhen, schnekhen ℨj: | **[Rubric] Consumption [Phthisis].** Water-snails, [dried] snails 1 drachm [ℨj]: *— Note: Continues across column break to col. B, l. 1.* |

### Column B (Right Column, within red rules X=2776..5157, Y=274..6498)

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | ist ein khöstliches mitl wider die dör vnd | is a precious remedy against consumption and *— Note: Completes sentence from col. A, l. 43.* |
| 2 | lungl sucht, morgens vnd abents auf 1 M[esserspitz] woll | pulmonary phthisis, taken morning and evening by the knife-point |
| 3 | Suppen genomen. | in broth. |
| 4 | **[Rubric] Schwinden der glider — oder** | **[Rubric] Wasting [atrophy] of the limbs — or** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 5 | **[Rubric] grossen schmerzen der Nerven.** | **[Rubric] severe pains of the nerves.** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 6 | ℞ der rothen waldt schnekhen, souill du haben khanst, | Take [℞] red forest slugs [Arion rufus], as many as you can get, |
| 7 | thue in ein Zügkhern glaß gethan, mach mit 🜔 [Salz] schicht | put them into a confection-jar, layer them with salt [🜔] [stratum super stratum], *— Note: Zügkhern glaß = Zuckerglas (wide-mouthed apothecary jar).* |
| 8 | vnd Verbindt es woll, sez 14 tag in ein warmes | bind it up well, set it for 14 days in a warm |
| 9 | hauffen, so werden die schnekhen zergehen, vnd zu | [dung-]heap [digestion in fimo equino], and the slugs will dissolve and turn into |
| 10 | einen öll, seichs durch ein duch, vnd behalts, las | an oil [liquor limacum]; strain it through a cloth and keep it; let |
| 11 | dier lieb sein, dan wo Nerven ein schrumpffen | it be dear to you, for wherever nerves/sinews contract |
| 12 | oder ein glidt schwind, oder schmerzen in | or a limb wastes away, or there are pains in |
| 13 | Nerven sein, so schmier täglich 2 mall. | the nerves, anoint therewith twice daily. |
| 14 | **[Rubric] Dörsichtigen** | **[Rubric] For the consumptive [phthisical patients]** *— Note: Red rubric.* |
| 15 | Die ganzen schnekhen mit den heislein, sÿe | Whole snails with their shells |
| 16 | seind hard zu verdäuen, der aber ein guetten | are hard to digest, but to one who has a good |
| 17 | Magen hat, dem geben sÿe ein reiche vnd | stomach they provide a rich and |
| 18 | wolkhumbne nahrung, dero wegen seind sÿe | welcome nourishment; therefore they are |
| 19 | denen dörsichtigen, beÿ denen die Natirliche | for consumptive patients, in whom the natural |
| 20 | khräfften garz dörrt mit Putter gekhocht | powers are wholly parched, when boiled with butter, |
| 21 | sehr khöstlich. oder machs also. | most precious. Or prepare it thus: |

## Page 32 — IIIF Canvas #9

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0009.JP2` (4977 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0009.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0009.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 32 (recto, numbered '32' in upper-right margin; faces p. 30 on verso Canvas 8, skipping '31' in pagination without text loss). Column A completes the recipe from p. 30, then gives an important historical note on Balsam of Copaiba ('Balsamo Copauia') recently imported from the Indies ('auß India') and used for remarkable cures in Vienna ('in Wienn' — confirming the manuscript's Austrian provenance), followed by surgical management of carious jawbone (incision, actual cautery, oil of vitriol, exfoliation) and syringe-injections for dental fistulae. Column B features a half-column watercolour bust portrait of a mustachioed gentleman in a red doublet and wide pleated ruff, above remedies for putrefaction/ulceration of the mouth ('Von fälung des Mundts', stomatitis/scurvy), including a pauper's remedy ('arme leüd die nichts Vermögen') of watercress, scurvy-grass, and horseradish.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Watercolour Bust Portrait of a Gentleman in Red Doublet and Pleated Ruff (p. 32, Col. B Upper)** (`#xywh=2501,287,2255,2993`): Half-column watercolour portrait showing a dark-haired, mustachioed man in a crimson doublet and broad scalloped lace-edged ruff Collar (representing a patient afflicted with oral/dental disease), bordered below by a double red-and-ochre rule.

## Page 33 — IIIF Canvas #10

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0010.JP2` (5423 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0010.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0010.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 33 (verso, numbered '33' in upper-left margin). Column A treats scraping necrotic gum roots with a razor ('schar mäser'), incarnative powders to make rotted gums grow back, tightening loose teeth ('Zant wagel'), a potent sublimate-and-alum water applied with a small screw/swab, and remedies for halitosis. Column B treats inflammation and swelling of the tonsils and uvula ('Mandl vnd Zäpffel'), including lead acetate ('bleÿ Zukher') gargles and the folk-surgical maneuver of pulling the patient hard by the earlobes to raise a fallen uvula ('Wan das Zäpffel abgefallen ziehe ihm Woll beÿ den ohr läpel'), followed by a watercolour drawing of four multi-rooted human molar teeth and the opening of the treatise on toothache ('Von den Zänten Zuestänten vnd khrankheiten').

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Watercolour Study of Four Extracted Human Molar Teeth (p. 33, Col. B Middle)** (`#xywh=2850,3720,2200,930`): Shaded watercolour anatomical illustration of four extracted human molars in lateral/oblique view, depicting crown cusps and bifurcated/trifurcated roots, above a double yellow-and-red horizontal rule.

## Page 34 — IIIF Canvas #11

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0011.JP2` (5031 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0011.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0011.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 34 (recto, numbered '34' in upper-right margin). Continues the treatise on toothache ('Von Zant schmerzen') from p. 33. Distinguishes hot vs. cold catarrhal fluxions, observes that extraction ('herauß reiß') is the only permanent cure for carious teeth, and provides anodyne cotton-wool pellets, aural instillation of castoreum or horseradish juice, temporal plasters (Gum Elemi, mastic, opium), Laudanum opiatum, camphor ('XXX'), guaiacum-wine solar digests, and astrological/solar remedies using ash-wood ('lign. fraxino') gathered on St. John's Day (Midsummer) before sunrise ('Vor auf gang der ☉') and sorrel gathered in June before sunrise.

## Page 35 — IIIF Canvas #12

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0012.JP2` (5417 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0012.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0012.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 35 (verso, numbered '35' in upper-left margin). Column A covers remedies for carious teeth, non-surgical chemical shedding of rotten teeth ('Das die Zant auß falen') using mulberry root steeped in strong vinegar, tooth-preserving and whitening powders (burnt hartshorn, mastic, calcined salt), and infant teething remedies ('Das den khindern die Zant Paldt Wachsen') using butter, honey, and hare's brain ('hasen hirn') rubbed on the gums ('Zant Piller'). Column B opens with a watercolour illustration of a spotted frog ('Fröschel')—a visual pun on sublingual ranula ('Von Fröschel vnder der Zungen / ranula', literally 'little frog')—followed by medical and surgical treatment of ranula (astringent gargles, lancet incision, or venesection of the sublingual vein).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Watercolour Illustration of a Spotted Frog ('Fröschel' / Ranula Visual Mnemonic, p. 35, Col. B Upper)** (`#xywh=2819,243,2370,1787`): Watercolour painting of a brown-and-ochre spotted frog in dorsal view with four webbed feet, serving as a visual-etymological emblem for 'Fröschel vnder der Zungen' (Latin 'ranula', 'little frog', sublingual retention cyst/swelling), above a double yellow-ochre rule.

## Page 36 — IIIF Canvas #13

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0013.JP2` (5047 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0013.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0013.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 36 (recto, numbered '36' in upper-right margin). Upper Column A displays a watercolour study of four human teeth (single-, two-, and three-rooted) above a candid first-person clinical warning on treating loose teeth ('Rogle Zant föst machen'): 'one reaps more disgrace than honor therewith... if it does not help you, do not blame me, blame your own sour scorbutic blood!'. Includes astringent electuaries, Croll's Lapis medicamentosus with Gum Lac, and live crayfish pounded with burdock root to regenerate gums. Column B treats tooth whitening and features a watercolour profile portrait of a patient injecting his mouth with a brass syringe, introducing 'Vnderschidtliche Mundt oder Einspritz Wasser' (mouth- and syringe-waters for loose teeth and mercurial salivation).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Watercolour Study of Four Single- and Multi-Rooted Human Teeth (p. 36, Col. A Upper)** (`#xywh=257,282,2286,1168`): Watercolour anatomical row of four extracted human teeth showing a single-rooted premolar, conical canine/incisor, bifurcated molar, and trifurcated molar above a double yellow-ochre rule.

**Watercolour Profile Portrait of a Patient Using a Brass Oral Syringe (p. 36, Col. B Middle)** (`#xywh=2543,3780,2281,1620`): Watercolour half-length profile of a male patient wearing a striped cap and red tunic with a turned brass wound/oral syringe ('Einspritz-Spritze') inserted into his mouth, above a double yellow-ochre rule.

## Page 37 — IIIF Canvas #14

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0014.JP2` (5352 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0014.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0014.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 37 (verso, numbered '37' in upper-left margin). Column A completes the syringe-injection water from p. 36, then gives a comprehensive Tormentil-root gargle for scurvy ('Mund fäul Scharbokh'), a honey-electuary for eroded gums, and a compound herbal mixture to be kept dried in a box ('in einer geschachtl behalten') for instant preparation of gargle-waters. Column B gives insufflation and gargle remedies for a prolapsed uvula (blowing celandine root powder, nitre '🜕', or sugar of lead 'Sachari ♄'), tonsillar abscesses, deep throat ulcers ('dieffe löcher im hals'), and mercurial salivation using oak bark and Album Graecum.

## Page 38 — IIIF Canvas #15

**Folio / Side:** 19v · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0015.JP2` (5041 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0015.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0015.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 38 (fol. 19v) concludes a gargle recipe with lignum sanctum and mulberry juice, followed by caustic precipitate waters, syringe waters for putrid ulcers and nasal sores, mouthwashes for putrid gums, and a remedy for chilled throat fluxes. Below a colored portrait of a bearded man in a turban-like cap and neckcloth, a major section begins on 'Angina der Hals Enge' (quinsy / throat constriction), describing the anatomy of the esophagus and trachea.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait of a man with neckcloth** (`#xywh=2620,2670,1920,1130`): Watercolor and ink bust portrait of a mustachioed man wearing a dark brimmed cap/turban, a dark scarf/neckcloth wrapped around his throat, and a reddish-brown buttoned coat, illustrating the section on throat ailments (Angina).

## Page 39 — IIIF Canvas #16

**Folio / Side:** 20r · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0016.JP2` (5501 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0016.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0016.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 39 (fol. 20r) continues the treatise on Angina, distinguishing true 'Angina exquisita' (with high fever, thirst, and mortal danger of suffocation) from 'Angina spuria' (false angina). It provides prognostic signs ('NB: Pronostico') and outlines the urgent therapeutic regimen ('Die Chur'), emphasizing immediate clysters, repeated bloodletting (median vein, leeches, sublingual veins), and warm foot/arm baths.

## Page 40 — IIIF Canvas #17

**Folio / Side:** 20v · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0017.JP2` (5104 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0017.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0017.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 40 (fol. 20v) continues the treatment of Angina with gentle cassia and tamarind laxatives, cooling initial gargle waters (with nightshade, rose water, and vinegar), resolving and ripening poultices/plasters (using marshmallow root, figs, swallow's nest, and lily root), and gargle waters and cupping glasses ('Ventosen') to break the aposteme and relieve severe throat pain and dyspnea.

## Page 41 — IIIF Canvas #18

**Folio / Side:** 21r · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0018.JP2` (5521 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0018.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0018.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Treatises and recipes for parotid swellings (Parotides, apostems behind the ears) and scrofula/glandular swellings (Scrofulae, Glandulae, Bubones) on the neck, illustrated with two bust portraits.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait of a young man in profile** (`#xywh=1250,280,1650,1270`): Bust portrait in left profile of a young man with short brown hair wearing a yellow and green/grey striped collar/garment, above a horizontal rule.

**Frontal portrait of a youth** (`#xywh=3100,4600,1750,1300`): Frontal bust portrait of a youth with curly brown hair wearing a red, green, and dark-striped collar/garment, above a horizontal rule.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 41 | 41 *— Note: Original page number in upper-left box; '39' appears on the left stub/margin.* |
| 2 | **[Rubric] Von den Perotide.** | On Parotides. *— Note: Parotid gland swellings (parotitis).* |
| 3 | **[Rubric] apostem hinder den ohren.** | Apostems behind the ears. |

| 12 | wölliche mit dem hirren vereiniget seind. | which are united with the brain. |
| 13 | seind alß dan wegen gefährlich wegen anderer | They are therefore dangerous on account of other *— Note: Dittography of 'wegen'.* |

## Page 42 — IIIF Canvas #19

**Folio / Side:** 21v · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0019.JP2` (5003 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0019.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0019.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

## Page 43 — IIIF Canvas #20

**Folio / Side:** 22r · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0020.JP2` (5481 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0020.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0020.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Continuation of the wondrous plaster, caustic sublimate granules (granela) for unopened scrofula with a caution against sharp instruments, followed by St. Dominic's remedy using pyrethrum and sharp-leaved dock, purgatives, and an electuary (Lattwergen) with calomel.

## Page 44 — IIIF Canvas #21

**Folio / Side:** 22v · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0021.JP2` (5104 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0021.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0021.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Treatise and remedies for goitre (Strumae / Halskropf): includes an illustration of two figures with goitres, a theoretical introduction on types and curability of goitres, and multiple medicinal powder and calcination recipes.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Goitre Dialogue** (`#xywh=270,150,2280,1610`): Watercolor illustration of a man in a green turban and a woman with a braided headdress, both displaying large neck goitres and pointing at each other's swellings, with German caption above.

## Page 46 — IIIF Canvas #23

**Folio / Side:** 23v · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0023.JP2` (5137 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0023.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0023.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Treatise on abscesses and ulcers of the ear ('Von apostem vnd geschwer der ohrn'), detailing causes, symptoms, and treatments including ear washes, decoctions, and ointments, followed by a section with remedies for red, brown, or yellow birthmarks ('Vor die Rotte, braune oder gelbe Muttermaill').

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fig. 1 (Ear abscess patient)** (`#xywh=200,140,2430,1330`): Bust of a young person in profile facing right, raising one hand to touch his cheek/ear area, above a framed ledge marked '1'.

**Fig. 2 (Birthmark patients)** (`#xywh=2620,4650,2430,1500`): Front-facing bust of a woman with reddish-brown birthmarks on her forehead/right eye and under her left eye, wearing a headscarf, with a small child standing behind her left shoulder, above a framed ledge marked '||'.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |

## Page 47 — IIIF Canvas #24

**Folio / Side:** 24r · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0024.JP2` (5541 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0024.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0024.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Continuation of remedies for birthmarks (distillate, mercury/bolus, alabaster ointment, and folk remedies using placenta or oak-fire water), followed by a new illustrated section on facial pimples/pustules ('finen in angesicht oder Vnder der handt'), detailing causes and topical washes/mixtures.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fig. 1 (Facial pimples/pustules patient)** (`#xywh=380,4620,2390,1360`): Front-facing head of a woman wearing a light headscarf/bonnet, showing reddish spots/pimples on her cheeks and face, above a framed ledge marked '|||'.

## Page 48 — IIIF Canvas #25

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0025.JP2` (5283 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0025.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0025.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 48 (recto, numbered '48' in upper-right margin; opening anatomical plate of the Ophthalmology treatise). Features watercolour diagrams of the extraocular muscles, isolated ocular humors, and a multi-layered physical paper flap dissection ('fugitive sheet' / anatomical volvelle) of the human eye in Column A (whose lifted states are photographed on Canvases 727–730), paired with an anatomical treatise in Column B explaining labels A–R and the origin of the seven tunics from the cerebral meninges.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Diagram A: Eye in its form with extraocular muscles elevated** (`#xywh=250,280,1270,1280`): Watercolour drawing of the eyeball ('A') with the rectus muscles splayed upward toward the optic apex.

**Diagram A–I, a: Posterior/frontal stellate view of the six extraocular muscles, trochlea G, sclera H, optic nerve I, and motor nerve twigs a** (`#xywh=1220,250,1320,1830`): Watercolour anatomical rosette showing muscles A–F radiating from globe H/I, pulley G on the superior oblique F, and nerve branches a.

**Diagram B: Crystalline humor with the tunica aranea** (`#xywh=450,1820,460,410`): Concentric circular watercolour diagram of the lens encircled by the arachnoid/ciliary membrane.

**Diagram D: Crystalline humor bare and alone** (`#xywh=520,2470,270,270`): Small circular watercolour diagram of the isolated crystalline lens ('D').

**Diagram C: Posterior white vitreous humor with the crystalline lens** (`#xywh=1950,2120,420,320`): Crescentic watercolour diagram of the vitreous body ('C') cradling the spherical crystalline lens.

**Laminated Multi-Layer Flap Dissection of the Eye (Labels A–R across 4 physical states, Canvases 25 & 727–730)** (`#xywh=910,1520,1450,2270`): Interactive 3-flap watercolour anatomical cut-out pasted onto p. 48, lifting sequentially to reveal Tunica Conjunctiva/Cornea (F, G, H), Tunica Uvea/Choroid (I, K, R), Tunica Retina (L), and the internal humors (m, n, o, P).

| 5 | Die hinder weisse feicht= | C. The posterior white humor, *— Note: Caption beneath middle-right inset C (line 1 of 3).* |
| 6 | heit Vitreus mit | the vitreous, with *— Note: Caption beneath inset C (line 2 of 3).* |
| 7 | dem Cristalinum | the crystalline [lens]. *— Note: Caption beneath inset C (line 3 of 3).* |

### Column A Lower (Key to the Extraocular Muscles A–I, a and the 7 Tunics, below red rule X=220..2551, Y=3848..6464)

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | A das erste aufhebente hochfertige meislein | A. The first lifting, 'haughty' muscle [superior rectus, musculus attollens/superbus]. *— Note: Traditional physiognomic names of the recti muscles.* |
| 2 | B. das erste Nider drukhente meislein diemietige | B. The first depressing, 'humble' muscle [inferior rectus, musculus deprimens/humilis]. |
| 3 | C das erste an ziehente meislein. | C. The first drawing-in [adducting] muscle [medial rectus, musculus adducens]. |
| 4 | D das erste abziehente meislein. | D. The first drawing-away [abducting] muscle [lateral rectus, musculus abducens]. |
| 5 | E das vnder schlimme meislein des herumb. | E. The lower oblique muscle that winds around [inferior oblique, musculus obliquus inferior]. *— Note: schlimme = schräge (oblique).* |
| 6 | F das obere schlimme meislein | F. The upper oblique muscle [superior oblique, musculus obliquus superior]. |
| 7 | G die augen Roll dises meislein. | G. The eye-pulley [trochlea] of this muscle. |

| 9 | das aug bedökht. | covers the eye. |

| 11 | a a a a a a. der augen span adern, welliche den | a a a a a a. The motor nerves of the eyes, which *— Note: Cranial nerves III, IV, VI supplying the extraocular muscles.* |
| 12 | augen ihr bewegung, khrafft vnd empfindtligkhait auß den | convey to the eyes their movement, power, and sensitivity out of the |
| 13 | gehirn zue fiehret | brain. |

## Page 49 — IIIF Canvas #26

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0026.JP2` (5532 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0026.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0026.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg)

Page 49 (verso, numbered '49' in upper-left margin; full-width single-column layout across the red-ruled frame, cited in Wellcome catalogue 'Pp. 49-165 are devoted to ophthalmology'). Completes the sentence from p. 48 col. B l. 53 ('genand Cornea'), then treats: (1) The Three Humors of the Eye (Vitreus, Crystallinus, Albugineus/Aquosus, noting that cataract arises in the anterior aqueous humor); (2) The Six Extraocular Muscles (noting the Vesalian observation that the 7th retractor bulbi muscle exists only in beasts/cattle and not in humans); and (3) A scholastic Latin-German synthesis of the parts, nerves, vessels, glands, and tunics constituting the eye.

## Page 50 — IIIF Canvas #27

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0027.JP2` (5202 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0027.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0027.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 50 (recto, numbered '50' in upper-right margin; continuing the Ophthalmology Treatise from p. 49). Column A completes the description of the Cornea/Sclera, describes the Uvea/Choroid (originating from the pia mater), the Pupil and ciliary interstice, and cites Jean Riolan (Anthropographia lib. 4, cap. 6) on why only two universal tunics (Cornea and Uvea) truly merit the name Tunica. Column B describes the Aranea (capsule), Retina, and Vitreous tunic, then opens the section 'Von den humoribus der Augen Vnd wie das gesicht gemacht wirdt' (Of the Humors of the Eyes and How Vision is Produced), detailing the aqueous and crystalline humors. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 51 — IIIF Canvas #28

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0028.JP2` (5496 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0028.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0028.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 51 (verso, numbered '51' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the anatomical physiology of the crystalline and vitreous humors and explains the Galenic-Renaissance theory of vision ('Visio das gesicht') as the meeting of external visible species with internal visual spirits flowing down the optic nerve. Column B opens the clinical ophthalmology section with a bold red rubric ('Hier fang ich an, in Namen Gottes Von den khrankheiten der augen, Jnerlich vnd Eüserlich'), citing Pliny the Elder's attribution of 365 diseases to the eye alone and framing blindness with the proverb 'Ain Blinder Man — ain armer Man'. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 52 — IIIF Canvas #29

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0029.JP2` (5185 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0029.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0029.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 52 (recto, numbered '52' in upper-right margin). Column A features a large half-column watercolour portrait of a young man in a crimson gold-trimmed cap exhibiting bilateral pale/cloudy cataract pupils, above a red-rubric classification of the four principal causes of eye disease ('Die khrankheiten der augen bestehen in 4 Vrsachen'). Column B opens the clinical treatise on cataracts ('Erstens Was der Staan oder Cataracta ist / Seÿ' and 'Wie die Cataracta Erwachssen vnd in die augen khumen'), describing the five colors of cataract (white, gray, yellow, green, blue), noting that white and gray cataracts are the most common and easiest to couch/cure by an experienced oculist ('Erfahrnen Oculisten'). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait of a Patient with Bilateral Cataracts (p. 52, Col. A Upper)** (`#xywh=200,200,2340,2980`): Half-column watercolour bust portrait of a young mustachioed man wearing a crimson cap with broad gold-ochre trim and a dark high-collared doublet, depicting pale, cloudy pupils characteristic of cataracts ('der Staan').

## Page 53 — IIIF Canvas #30

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0030.JP2` (5463 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0030.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0030.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 53 (verso, numbered '53' in upper-left margin). Column A enumerates six internal and external causes of cataracts (brain, phlegmatic blood, foul stomach/spleen/liver vapors, coarse foods like onions, garlic, horseradish, mustard, smoked meat, peas, and lentils, excessive weeping, and trauma), and refutes the common belief that a cataract must take 7 years to ripen, citing the author's own clinical experience curing patients blind for only 6 months to 2 years. Column B lists the prodromal signs of cataract ('Von den Zaichen des Staan': cobwebs, floaters/flies, nets, fog, colored halos, diplopia, congenital cataract) and explains how to distinguish gastric vs. cranial origin ('die Vrsach Zuerkhenen ob sÿe Seÿ auß dem Magen oder haubt') by fluctuation with meals, time of day, lunar phase, and weather. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

Page 54 (recto, numbered '54' in upper-right margin). Column A concludes the diagnostic signs of gastric vs. cranial cataract, then opens 'Von den Weisen Milchstar' (Of the White Milky Cataract) with a large half-column watercolour portrait (#1) of a bearded Franciscan friar in his brown hooded habit exhibiting bilateral white milky pupils. Column B treats 'Wie Man den Weisen Star Vor khumen vnd begegnen Soll' (preventive regimen and purgation with maidenhair fern, agaric, dodder, senna, and myrobalans, plus Panis Laxativus and Pilulae Hiera Picra, and a compound electuary). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait #1: Franciscan Friar with White Milky Cataracts (p. 54, Col. A Lower)** (`#xywh=200,2980,2320,3100`): Half-column watercolour bust portrait labeled '#.1.' showing a bearded Franciscan friar in a brown hooded habit (cowl) with wide-open eyes and opaque white pupils illustrating the 'Weisen Milchstar' (milky cataract).

## Page 55 — IIIF Canvas #32

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0032.JP2` (5454 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0032.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0032.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 55 (verso, numbered '55' in upper-left margin). Column A gives a steam-fumigation ('Ain Guette Bähung Vor den Weißen Starn') using eyebright, white lily, valerian, fennel, and basil, paired with a half-column watercolour illustration ('Figur Wie Man sich Zum Bähen Schikhen soll') depicting a patient in a green coat with red cuffs holding a steaming pot under a white linen cloth tent. Column B records sympathetic/amuletic wearing of Verbena (for white/green cataracts in men, yellow for women) and blue chicory root dug on St. John's Day in the waning moon, then opens 'Von den Grauen Star' (Of the Gray Cataract) and its preventive powder and fumigation. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Figur: Patient Performing Ocular Steam Fumigation (p. 55, Col. A Lower)** (`#xywh=400,3180,2250,3300`): Half-column watercolour illustration titled 'Figur Wie Man sich Zum Bähen Schikhen soll' showing a patient in a green coat with red cuffs and grey stockings leaning over a steaming earthenware pot on a yellow wooden table with a white linen cloth draped over the head to trap the herbal vapor.

## Page 60 — IIIF Canvas #33

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0033.JP2` (5196 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0033.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0033.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 60 (recto, numbered '60' in upper-right margin; pagination jumps from 55 to 60 without textual loss). Column A completes the steaming recipe from p. 55, displays Portrait No. 2 ('Figur des Grauben Staan', showing gray-ash cataract pupils), and adds a second steaming recipe ('Ein Andere Bähung Vor den grauen Starn') and the start of an eye-water. Column B completes the green copper/sal-ammoniac eye-water with an inlaid watercolour diagram of wick-filtration/decanting through a red felt strip ('filzl rodes düch') into a glass flask, followed by an alembic-distilled eye-water and an electuary ('Volgente Lattwerung Ist Sehr khöstlich') to strengthen the eyes and clear the optic nerves. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait No. 2: Patient with Gray Cataract (p. 60, Col. A Middle)** (`#xywh=200,880,2320,3100`): Half-column watercolour bust portrait labeled 'No. 2.' and titled 'Figur des Grauben Staan', depicting a curly-haired figure with light gray/ash-colored pupils.

**Apparatus Diagram: Wick-Filtration / Decanting into Glass Flask (p. 60, Col. B Upper)** (`#xywh=2560,1680,1320,1240`): Watercolour laboratory diagram showing a cylindrical basin on a wooden block decanting green-tinted eye-water through a red felt strip ('filzl rodes düch') into a globular glass flask on a lower block.

## Page 61 — IIIF Canvas #34

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0034.JP2` (5482 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0034.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0034.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 61 (verso, numbered '61' in upper-left margin). Column A prescribes an early regimen to prevent cataracts ('den Starn Züeverhindern') combining cranial purgations (Extractum Catholicum, diagrydium pills), venesection, tea/decoction ('Thee vnd Chaus' = tea and coffee/cacao?), and vitrum antimonii/camphor collyria. Column B finishes a collyrium, presents a famous eye-water credited with curing a man blind for 9 years ('Volgentes Wasser hat ainen geholffen der 9 Jahr blindt war'), and opens the clinical manual on examining cataract patients ('Volgt Wie Man Guett vnd bösse Menge der augen Underschiden Soll')—instructing the oculist to interrogate the patient carefully before couching. Transcribed with Gemini 4.
